Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up time that someone else might need more. Um... I guess I'm here because... well, physically, I've just been feeling a little tight lately. It's almost like I'm bracing for a classroom observation that never ends, you know? I teach kindergarten, so I try to stay really positive and relaxed for the kids, but by the evening, my jaw aches so badly I can hardly even enjoy playing my guitar. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I suppose "overwhelmed" might be a bit of a strong word. I mean, everyone gets a little stressed, right? I just want to do a good job. But... I guess my mind has been kind of circling a lot lately. Just overthinking things, mostly. 

Like, I'll start worrying about whether my contract will be renewed and if I'll ever get hired again, or if I've somehow completely ruined my reputation with the parents. And then that just snowballs into worrying that my retirement is slipping away. It sounds so silly when I say it out loud, but... it gets to the point where if I make even a small mistake during a story-time activity, my brain just spirals and tells me that it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. 

Which I know is a terrible thing to think, because I really do love my students and I'm very careful with them, I promise. Am I making any sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent as a teacher. I'm sure it's pretty normal to worry about your career like that, isn't it?

Well, physically... um... I guess maybe my sleep hasn't been the absolute best lately? I mean, I know as you get older—I'm 53 now—you don't sleep quite as soundly, right? So I really don't want to make a big deal out of it or sound like I'm complaining.

I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I just want to make sure everything is perfect and in order, you know? And then, even when I finally do manage to drift off... I almost always wake up around four in the morning. And I'm already completely tense, just... calculating every single thing that could possibly go wrong that day. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like I can't handle my responsibilities. I still make sure I have plenty of energy for the kids, I promise! It's just a little bit of lost sleep, which is pretty standard for teachers wanting to do a good job, don't you think?

I'm sure it's probably just normal stress, right? I just wanted to make sure I'm doing everything I can to stay healthy for my students. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose \"overwhelmed\" might be a bit of a strong word. I mean, everyone gets a little stressed, right? I just want to do a good job. But... I guess my mind has been kind of circling a lot lately. Just overthinking things, mostly. \n\nLike, I'll start worrying about whether my contract will be renewed and if I'll ever get hired again, or if I've somehow completely ruined my reputation with the parents. And then that just snowballs into worrying that my retirement is slipping away. Am I making any sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent as a teacher. I guess maybe my sleep hasn't been the absolute best lately? I mean, I know as you get older—I'm 53 now—you don't sleep quite as soundly, right? So I really don't want to make a big deal out of it or sound like I'm complaining. \n\nBut, um, most nights I do end up just lying in bed for hours. I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I just want to make sure everything is perfect and in order, you know? And then, even when I finally do manage to drift off... I almost always wake up around four in the morning. Oh, sad? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for the kids. They pick up on everything, you know? So I always make sure to have a smile on my face when I walk into the classroom. 

I guess I feel a little... drained sometimes? Just from all the worrying and the lack of sleep. But not depressed or down in the dumps or anything like that. I still love my job, and I still love playing my guitar when I can actually get my jaw to relax. I just want to make sure I'm doing my best, and I think that just takes a lot of mental energy. It's not sadness, just... maybe a bit of mental fatigue? I hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for the life I have, because I know I'm very lucky.

Well, um... I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. 

Like, um, with story-time... I used to always volunteer to lead it, but lately, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I still help out, of course! I just... I get so worried about picking the wrong book or messing up the activity that it's just easier if someone else takes the lead. And, um... I guess I've also sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines for permanent positions. Not on purpose, I swear! I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. And the interviews I have gone to... well, I get so nervous that I end up stumbling over my words a bit. 

I just... I really want to get back into the classroom full-time and be reliable, but it feels like I'm getting in my own way sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.

I'm sure it's pretty normal to worry about your career like that, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other physical symptoms besides the tension in your shoulders and neck?\n\nPatient: Well, physically... um... I guess maybe my sleep hasn't been the absolute best lately? I mean, I know as you get older—I'm 53 now—you don't sleep quite as soundly, right? So I really don't want to make a big deal out of it or sound like I'm complaining. \n\nBut, um, most nights I do end up just lying in bed for hours. I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I just want to make sure everything is perfect and in order, you know? And then, even when I finally do manage to drift off... I almost always wake up around four in the morning. And I'm already completely tense, just... calculating every single thing that could possibly go wrong that day. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I can't handle my responsibilities. I still make sure I have plenty of energy for the kids, I promise! It's just a little bit of lost sleep, which is pretty standard for teachers wanting to do a good job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for the kids. They pick up on everything, you know? So I always make sure to have a smile on my face when I walk into the classroom. \n\nI guess I feel a little... drained sometimes? Just from all the worrying and the lack of sleep. But not depressed or down in the dumps or anything like that. I still love my job, and I still love playing my guitar when I can actually get my jaw to relax. I just want to make sure I'm doing my best, and I think that just takes a lot of mental energy. It's not sadness, just... maybe a bit of mental fatigue? I hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for the life I have, because I know I'm very lucky.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. \n\nLike, um, with story-time... I used to always volunteer to lead it, but lately, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I still help out, of course! I just... I get so worried about picking the wrong book or messing up the activity that it's just easier if someone else takes the lead. And, um... I guess I've also sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines for permanent positions. Not on purpose, I swear! I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. And the interviews I have gone to... well, I get so nervous that I end up stumbling over my words a bit. \n\nI just... I really want to get back into the classroom full-time and be reliable, but it feels like I'm getting in my own way sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding any social interactions outside of work because you're worried about what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I mean... I don't think so? I really try to be friendly and outgoing, especially since I work with kids and parents all day. Well, um... I suppose I do think about those things. I just want to be responsible and prepared, you know? But... I guess maybe it does get a little heavy sometimes. 

My mind just... it kind of keeps circling. Like, I'll start wondering if I'll ever actually get hired for a permanent spot again, and then suddenly I'm worrying that maybe I've already ruined my reputation with the parents somehow, even though they always smile at me. And then it turns into stressing about whether my retirement is just slipping away entirely... I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? 

But then it even gets into the little things. Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. 

Does that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?

I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I just want to make sure everything is perfect and in order, you know? And then, even when I finally do manage to drift off... I almost always wake up around four in the morning. And I'm already completely tense, just... calculating every single thing that could possibly go wrong that day. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I can't handle my responsibilities. I still make sure I have plenty of energy for the kids, I promise! It's just a little bit of lost sleep, which is pretty standard for teachers wanting to do a good job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for the kids. They pick up on everything, you know? So I always make sure to have a smile on my face when I walk into the classroom. \n\nI guess I feel a little... drained sometimes? Just from all the worrying and the lack of sleep. But not depressed or down in the dumps or anything like that. I still love my job, and I still love playing my guitar when I can actually get my jaw to relax. I just want to make sure I'm doing my best, and I think that just takes a lot of mental energy. It's not sadness, just... maybe a bit of mental fatigue? I hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for the life I have, because I know I'm very lucky.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. \n\nLike, um, with story-time... I used to always volunteer to lead it, but lately, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I still help out, of course! I just... I get so worried about picking the wrong book or messing up the activity that it's just easier if someone else takes the lead. And, um... I guess I've also sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines for permanent positions. Not on purpose, I swear! I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything! I actually really love being around people. Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. 

But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. I just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I wouldn't say I *avoid* things, exactly. I mean, I still love what I do! I really do. But... I guess maybe I have pulled back just a tiny bit on a few things. Just to be safe, you know?

Like, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. 

And, um... I guess with the job hunt, things haven't been going as smoothly as I'd hoped. I've sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines. Not on purpose! I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the "right" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. 

So I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. 

I mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. 

I just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up time that someone else might need more. Um... Honestly, what finally pushed me to make the appointment is that my shoulders and neck just seem to stay clenched all day long. It's almost like I'm bracing for a classroom observation that never ends, you know? I teach kindergarten, so I try to stay really positive and relaxed for the kids, but by the evening, my jaw aches so badly I can hardly even enjoy playing my guitar. I'm sure it's probably just normal stress, right? I just wanted to make sure I'm doing everything I can to stay healthy for my students. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose \"overwhelmed\" might be a bit of a strong word. Am I making any sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent as a teacher. I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I still make sure I have plenty of energy for the kids, I promise! I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. \n\nLike, um, with story-time... I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. And the interviews I have gone to... well, I get so nervous that I end up stumbling over my words a bit. \n\nI just... I really want to get back into the classroom full-time and be reliable, but it feels like I'm getting in my own way sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding any social interactions outside of work because you're worried about what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I mean... I don't think so? I really try to be friendly and outgoing, especially since I work with kids and parents all day. You have to be approachable, right? \n\nI guess I haven't been going out a ton lately, but that's really just because I've been so tired from the sleep stuff we talked about, and... well, just trying to focus on getting my applications perfect. It's not that I'm worried people are judging me or anything like that. I just... I like to be prepared and rested, you know? \n\nI still talk to my friends and everything! Maybe just more over text right now, but I definitely don't avoid people because I'm worried about what they think. I really hope I'm explaining that right... I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything! I actually really love being around people. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like your job or finances, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I do think about those things. I just want to be responsible and prepared, you know? But... I guess maybe it does get a little heavy sometimes. \n\nMy mind just... it kind of keeps circling. Like, I'll start wondering if I'll ever actually get hired for a permanent spot again, and then suddenly I'm worrying that maybe I've already ruined my reputation with the parents somehow, even though they always smile at me. And then it turns into stressing about whether my retirement is just slipping away entirely... I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? \n\nBut then it even gets into the little things. Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life or relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... Oh, no, no, it's not about being judged! I mean, I really like people, and I always try to get along with everyone. I'm not... I'm not worried about what people think of me in that way.

I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding any social interactions outside of work because you're worried about what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I mean... I don't think so? I really try to be friendly and outgoing, especially since I work with kids and parents all day. It's not that I'm worried people are judging me or anything like that. I just... I like to be prepared and rested, you know? \n\nI still talk to my friends and everything! I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything! I actually really love being around people. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like your job or finances, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use a strong word like that. I mean, I'm not... falling apart or anything! I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying all day or that I can't handle things. \n\nI always make sure to put on a happy face, especially for the kids at school. You kind of have to, you know? I just get a little... wound up, I guess. A bit tired from thinking so much. But I manage it! I really try to stay positive and keep moving forward. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I'm in actual distress. I'm doing okay, really. I hope that makes sense? I just really try hard not to be a complainer, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain social or professional situations because you fear others might judge you negatively?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, it's not about being judged! I mean, I really like people, and I always try to get along with everyone. I'm not... It's more about holding myself to a high standard than worrying about anyone else judging me. I want to be a good colleague and a good teacher. \n\nI'm not avoiding people or hiding away because I'm scared they'll think badly of me. I really try to be friendly and professional. I mean, I might think about a conversation for a second, just to make sure I was helpful, but I don't obsess over it or anything. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really do try my hardest to keep everything running smoothly. I mean, I want to be reliable, you know? But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, and I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information... things have been a little bit tricky lately. 

I wouldn't say I'm failing or anything! But, um, I have missed a couple of application deadlines recently. And when I did have an interview, I sort of... stumbled through it a bit. I just wanted it to be perfect, you know? And, well, with the story-time volunteering I usually do, I've had to step back just a tiny bit. I still love doing it, but I only do it now if someone else handles the planning part. 

So I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom as reliably as I'd like. But I'm really trying to get back on track! I want to be a good teacher again. I hope you don't think I'm just slacking off or giving up... I really am trying my best. Does that make sense?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F48.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up time that someone else might need more. Um... I guess I'm here because... well, physically, I've just been feeling a little tight lately. Honestly, what finally pushed me to make the appointment is that my shoulders and neck just seem to stay clenched all day long. It's almost like I'm bracing for a classroom observation that never ends, you know? I teach kindergarten, so I try to stay really positive and relaxed for the kids, but by the evening, my jaw aches so badly I can hardly even enjoy playing my guitar. I'm sure it's probably just normal stress, right? I just wanted to make sure I'm doing everything I can to stay healthy for my students. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose \"overwhelmed\" might be a bit of a strong word. I mean, everyone gets a little stressed, right? I just want to do a good job. But... I guess my mind has been kind of circling a lot lately. Just overthinking things, mostly. \n\nLike, I'll start worrying about whether my contract will be renewed and if I'll ever get hired again, or if I've somehow completely ruined my reputation with the parents. And then that just snowballs into worrying that my retirement is slipping away. It sounds so silly when I say it out loud, but... it gets to the point where if I make even a small mistake during a story-time activity, my brain just spirals and tells me that it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. \n\nWhich I know is a terrible thing to think, because I really do love my students and I'm very careful with them, I promise. Am I making any sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound completely incompetent as a teacher. I'm sure it's pretty normal to worry about your career like that, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other physical symptoms besides the tension in your shoulders and neck?\n\nPatient: Well, physically... um... I guess maybe my sleep hasn't been the absolute best lately? I mean, I know as you get older—I'm 53 now—you don't sleep quite as soundly, right? So I really don't want to make a big deal out of it or sound like I'm complaining. \n\nBut, um, most nights I do end up just lying in bed for hours. I try to rest, I really do, but my brain just keeps replaying things... like past interview answers, or worrying about bills, or going over all those strict state licensing forms for the classroom. I just want to make sure everything is perfect and in order, you know? And then, even when I finally do manage to drift off... I almost always wake up around four in the morning. And I'm already completely tense, just... calculating every single thing that could possibly go wrong that day. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I can't handle my responsibilities. I still make sure I have plenty of energy for the kids, I promise! It's just a little bit of lost sleep, which is pretty standard for teachers wanting to do a good job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for the kids. They pick up on everything, you know? So I always make sure to have a smile on my face when I walk into the classroom. \n\nI guess I feel a little... drained sometimes? Just from all the worrying and the lack of sleep. But not depressed or down in the dumps or anything like that. I still love my job, and I still love playing my guitar when I can actually get my jaw to relax. I just want to make sure I'm doing my best, and I think that just takes a lot of mental energy. It's not sadness, just... maybe a bit of mental fatigue? I hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for the life I have, because I know I'm very lucky.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. \n\nLike, um, with story-time... I used to always volunteer to lead it, but lately, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I still help out, of course! I just... I get so worried about picking the wrong book or messing up the activity that it's just easier if someone else takes the lead. And, um... I guess I've also sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines for permanent positions. Not on purpose, I swear! I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. And the interviews I have gone to... well, I get so nervous that I end up stumbling over my words a bit. \n\nI just... I really want to get back into the classroom full-time and be reliable, but it feels like I'm getting in my own way sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding any social interactions outside of work because you're worried about what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I mean... I don't think so? I really try to be friendly and outgoing, especially since I work with kids and parents all day. You have to be approachable, right? \n\nI guess I haven't been going out a ton lately, but that's really just because I've been so tired from the sleep stuff we talked about, and... well, just trying to focus on getting my applications perfect. It's not that I'm worried people are judging me or anything like that. I just... I like to be prepared and rested, you know? \n\nI still talk to my friends and everything! Maybe just more over text right now, but I definitely don't avoid people because I'm worried about what they think. I really hope I'm explaining that right... I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything! I actually really love being around people. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like your job or finances, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I do think about those things. I just want to be responsible and prepared, you know? But... I guess maybe it does get a little heavy sometimes. \n\nMy mind just... it kind of keeps circling. Like, I'll start wondering if I'll ever actually get hired for a permanent spot again, and then suddenly I'm worrying that maybe I've already ruined my reputation with the parents somehow, even though they always smile at me. And then it turns into stressing about whether my retirement is just slipping away entirely... I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? \n\nBut then it even gets into the little things. Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I *avoid* things, exactly. I mean, I still love what I do! I really do. But... I guess maybe I have pulled back just a tiny bit on a few things. Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... I guess with the job hunt, things haven't been going as smoothly as I'd hoped. I've sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines. Not on purpose! I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life or relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it bother anyone else. My relationships are good, I think! I always try to be there for my friends and family. \n\nBut... as far as my daily life goes... I suppose I did mention earlier that things have been a bit tricky with the job hunt. Like, missing those application deadlines because I'm just trying so hard to get the cover letter right... and then when I do get an interview, I get so flustered trying to be perfect that I just stumble through it. And, you know, stepping back from planning the story-time sessions at work... \n\nSo, I guess, if I'm looking at the big picture... maybe this worrying is keeping me from getting back into the classroom in a reliable way. I just... I want to do it right, you know? But I suppose it is getting in the way a bit more than I'd like to admit. I'm really trying to fix it, though! I want to be a productive, reliable teacher. Does it sound like I'm at least self-aware about it? I'm hoping that's a good first step.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use a strong word like that. I mean, I'm not... falling apart or anything! I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying all day or that I can't handle things. \n\nI always make sure to put on a happy face, especially for the kids at school. You kind of have to, you know? I just get a little... wound up, I guess. A bit tired from thinking so much. But I manage it! I really try to stay positive and keep moving forward. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I'm in actual distress. I'm doing okay, really. I hope that makes sense? I just really try hard not to be a complainer, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding certain social or professional situations because you fear others might judge you negatively?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, it's not about being judged! I'm not worried about what people think of me in that way. \n\nI just, well, like I was saying before, I just want to make sure I'm doing things right, you know? It's more about holding myself to a high standard than worrying about anyone else judging me. I want to be a good colleague and a good teacher. \n\nI'm not avoiding people or hiding away because I'm scared they'll think badly of me. I really try to be friendly and professional. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I *avoid* things, exactly. I mean, I still love what I do! I really do. But... I guess maybe I have pulled back just a tiny bit on a few things. Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... I guess with the job hunt, things haven't been going as smoothly as I'd hoped. I've sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines. Not on purpose! I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life or relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I always try to be there for my friends and family. \n\nBut... as far as my daily life goes... I suppose I did mention earlier that things have been a bit tricky with the job hunt. Well, um... I guess I do think about those things a fair bit. I mean, it's normal to want to be responsible, right? I just want to make sure I'm prepared for the future.

Like, I'll start wondering if I'll ever actually get hired for a permanent spot again, and then suddenly I'm worrying that maybe I've already ruined my reputation with the parents somehow, even though they always smile at me. And then it turns into stressing about whether my retirement is just slipping away entirely... I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? \n\nBut then it even gets into the little things. Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I *avoid* things, exactly. I mean, I still love what I do! I really do. But... I guess maybe I have pulled back just a tiny bit on a few things. Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... I guess with the job hunt, things haven't been going as smoothly as I'd hoped. I've sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines. Not on purpose! I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life or relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it bother anyone else. My relationships are good, I think! I always try to be there for my friends and family. \n\nBut... as far as my daily life goes... I suppose I did mention earlier that things have been a bit tricky with the job hunt. Does it sound like I'm at least self-aware about it? I'm hoping that's a good first step.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? Oh, um... I'm not worried about what people think of me in that way. \n\nI just, well, like I was saying before, I just want to make sure I'm doing things right, you know? It's more about holding myself to a high standard than worrying about anyone else judging me. I want to be a good colleague and a good teacher. \n\nI'm not avoiding people or hiding away because I'm scared they'll think badly of me. I really try to be friendly and professional. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I know we sort of touched on this a moment ago, and I really hope I'm not repeating myself too much or taking up too much of your time. I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. 

I really do try to stay on top of my goals. I mean, getting back to teaching is so important to me. But... if I'm looking at it honestly, and I want to give you the most accurate picture... the worrying has made it a little tough to reach those goals lately. Like I mentioned before, I've ended up missing some application deadlines, and when I did have an interview, I just... I got so caught up in wanting it to go perfectly that I stumbled through it. 

And with the story-time volunteering, I really do still participate! But I did have to step back from the planning part, so I only do it if someone else organizes it now. So, I guess... yes, the anxiety is kind of keeping me from getting back into the classroom in a reliable way right now. But I'm really motivated to fix it! I want to be a good, dependable teacher again. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.

I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? \n\nBut then it even gets into the little things. Like, if I make just a small mistake in a story-time activity, I'll start thinking that maybe it means I shouldn't even be trusted with children anymore. Which is terrible to even say out loud, because I love my students and I'm always so careful with them! I just... I care so much about doing a perfect job that my brain just kind of runs away with all these worst-case scenarios. \n\nDoes that make sense? I really hope that doesn't make me sound incompetent or crazy. I promise I'm a good teacher, I just... I overthink things a bit. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. Just... humming in the background, you know? Like I'll wake up and immediately start going over my lesson plans in my head, making sure I haven't forgotten anything important. And even on the weekends, I might spend a little too much time thinking about the applications or if I handled a parent question the right way on Friday. \n\nI just... I really want to be on top of things, so I guess my brain just stays in that gear a lot. But I definitely have moments where I'm just relaxing! I try really hard to just breathe and be present. I hope it doesn't seem like I'm just a bundle of nerves all the time. I'm really trying to manage it well... does it sound like I'm handling it okay?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say I *avoid* things, exactly. I mean, I still love what I do! I really do. But... I guess maybe I have pulled back just a tiny bit on a few things. Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... I guess with the job hunt, things haven't been going as smoothly as I'd hoped. I've sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines. Not on purpose! I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. But I'm really trying to work on it! I'm still putting myself out there, I promise. I just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? It's just a little bit of lost sleep, which is pretty standard for teachers wanting to do a good job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Oh, sad? Um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I really try to keep a positive attitude, especially for the kids. They pick up on everything, you know? So I always make sure to have a smile on my face when I walk into the classroom. \n\nI guess I feel a little... drained sometimes? Just from all the worrying and the lack of sleep. But not depressed or down in the dumps or anything like that. I still love my job, and I still love playing my guitar when I can actually get my jaw to relax. I just want to make sure I'm doing my best, and I think that just takes a lot of mental energy. It's not sadness, just... maybe a bit of mental fatigue? I hope that's an okay answer. I really don't want to sound ungrateful for the life I have, because I know I'm very lucky.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any situations or activities because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it affect my work, because like I said, I want to do a good job. But... well, I suppose I have been holding back just a tiny bit lately. \n\nLike, um, with story-time... I used to always volunteer to lead it, but lately, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I still help out, of course! I just... I get so worried about picking the wrong book or messing up the activity that it's just easier if someone else takes the lead. And, um... I guess I've also sort of... missed a couple of application deadlines for permanent positions. Not on purpose, I swear! I just get so caught up in making sure my resume is absolutely perfect that I end up running out of time. And the interviews I have gone to... well, I get so nervous that I end up stumbling over my words a bit. \n\nI just... I really want to get back into the classroom full-time and be reliable, but it feels like I'm getting in my own way sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough. I really am trying, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding any social interactions outside of work because you're worried about what others might think of you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all! I mean... I don't think so? I really try to be friendly and outgoing, especially since I work with kids and parents all day. You have to be approachable, right? \n\nI guess I haven't been going out a ton lately, but that's really just because I've been so tired from the sleep stuff we talked about, and... well, just trying to focus on getting my applications perfect. It's not that I'm worried people are judging me or anything like that. I just... I like to be prepared and rested, you know? \n\nI still talk to my friends and everything! Maybe just more over text right now, but I definitely don't avoid people because I'm worried about what they think. I really hope I'm explaining that right... I don't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything! I actually really love being around people. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about everyday things, like your job or finances, even when things are going well?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I do think about those things. I just want to be responsible and prepared, you know? But... Like, I'll start wondering if I'll ever actually get hired for a permanent spot again, and then suddenly I'm worrying that maybe I've already ruined my reputation with the parents somehow, even though they always smile at me. I mean, I'm 53, so I have to be mindful of that, right? \n\nBut then it even gets into the little things. Is it normal to worry like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying almost every day?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I do worry a fair amount, but I wouldn't say it's every single day! I mean, some days are really good. When the kids are happy and engaged, I feel great. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there most of the time. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it bother anyone else. My relationships are good, I think! I always try to be there for my friends and family. \n\nBut... as far as my daily life goes... I suppose I did mention earlier that things have been a bit tricky with the job hunt. Like, missing those application deadlines because I'm just trying so hard to get the cover letter right... and then when I do get an interview, I get so flustered trying to be perfect that I just stumble through it. And, you know, stepping back from planning the story-time sessions at work... \n\nSo, I guess, if I'm looking at the big picture... maybe this worrying is keeping me from getting back into the classroom in a reliable way. I just... I want to do it right, you know? But I suppose it is getting in the way a bit more than I'd like to admit. I'm really trying to fix it, though! I want to be a productive, reliable teacher. Does it sound like I'm at least self-aware about it? I'm hoping that's a good first step.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Distress? Oh, um... That's the right way to look at it, isn't it? Just wanting to do a good job? I hope I'm explaining that clearly. I really don't want to sound like I'm overly sensitive or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself replaying conversations or events over and over in your head, thinking about how they could have gone better?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I try to be very present, especially for the kids. I think it's important to just keep a good attitude, right? I hope that's the kind of healthy mindset you're looking for. I really try my best to just be a good, supportive person and not get too caught up in my own head. Just to be safe, you know?\n\nLike, I used to always volunteer to lead the big story-time sessions, but lately... well, I've been asking if someone else can handle the planning part. I'll still help out! I just... I get so worried I'll mess up the schedule or pick the wrong book and disappoint everyone. It's just easier if someone else takes the lead right now. \n\nAnd, um... I just spend so much time trying to make my cover letter absolutely perfect that the time just slips away. And the interviews I have gone to... I get so flustered trying to give the \"right\" answer that I end up stumbling over my words. \n\nSo I guess... maybe the worrying is making it a little harder to get back into the classroom full-time. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm giving up, because I'm really not!\n\nDoctor: Are there any situations or activities that you're avoiding because you fear something specific might happen?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'm avoiding anything because of a specific fear, really. Like I said, it's mostly just... wanting to be careful and do things right. \n\nI mean, I don't have any, like, phobias or anything like that. I'm not scared of, you know, leaving the house or talking to people. It's just the everyday things that kind of pile up in my head. I'm not hiding away from the world, I promise! I'm still going to work every day and doing my best. \n\nI just... I want to be a good teacher and a responsible person, and sometimes that just means taking a step back to make sure I'm doing it perfectly. That's a good thing, right? To want to be careful? I really hope I'm not coming across as someone who just runs away from things.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is interfering with your daily life or relationships in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess... maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try not to let it bother anyone else. My relationships are good, I think! I always try to be there for my friends and family. \n\nBut... as far as my daily life goes... I suppose I did mention earlier that things have been a bit tricky with the job hunt. Like, missing those application deadlines because I'm just trying so hard to get the cover letter right... and then when I do get an interview, I get so flustered trying to be perfect that I just stumble through it. I want to be a good, dependable teacher again. I hope that's the kind of information you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to help me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you significant emotional pain or discomfort?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, I don't think I'd use the word pain.
